# Cayley discovery-credit exclusion; preserve formal certificate

Adopt the independent review's SUBSTANTIVE FAIL for MathDB 364119. The retained
published p.280 explicitly gives 33712 versus 33710 for patterns 1342 and 1423
at length seven. Both have length four and maximum four. The literal conjecture
on p.296 would require equality for such equal-maximum pairs. The primary body
therefore already supplies its literal negation. This supersedes r333's favorable
substantive assessment. It is a complete source-adaptation exclusion, not a claim
that a separately labelled refutation was previously published.

Retain the smaller 1122/1112 witness and unconditional Lean certificate, with the
reviewer's fresh 87-declaration replay. No additional discovery credit, manuscript
or root acceptance replay is warranted for this excluded candidate. Do not change
the problem to strict maximum or count the smaller parameter instance separately.
Specific-witness priority and global novelty remain bounded HOLD, independently
of the decisive credit objection.

Candidate audit-parser weaknesses are material: whole-declaration deletion,
missing types and duplicate reports could pass. The reviewer supplied independent
environment inventory and supplemental coverage checks, which must accompany any
future reuse of this formal packet. Preserve those checks, mutation evidence and
all failed predecessor runs. Reviewer pre-freeze SUMMARY reading is a disclosed
timing deviation, not retroactively repaired by matching hashes. Root verifies
payloads and actual original bindings, not a new Lean replay.

Coverage stays 583 reported statement reviews, counts 9 additional / 39 total /
next 40. Allocate Sol r364 a third disjoint breadth batch, excluding both active
sets: Sol r362 (root r361) and Claude r360 (root r359). Future reservations must
exclude all three active sets. No new hypothesis is manufactured to fill a slot.

ID 371030 Generalized restricted Marstrand conjecture for analytic linear families LINE 179
Let $P_t:\mathbb{R}^n\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^m$ be an analytic family of linear maps. Define $f:[0,n]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ as follows: for an integer $x\in[0,n]$, let $f(x)$ be the largest integer such that every subspace $A\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ with $\dim A=x$ satisfies

$$
\dim P_t(A)\geq f(x)
$$

for all but finitely many $t\in[0,1]$; for noninteger $x$, define $f$ linearly on $[\lfloor x\rfloor,\lfloor x\rfloor+1]$. **Generalized restricted Marstrand conjecture.** For every Borel set $A\subset\mathbb{R}^n$,

$$
\dim_H P_t(A)\geq f(\dim_H A)
$$

for almost every $t\in[0,1]$. This is a more general lower-bound formulation of the restricted Marstrand problem, allowing the expected projection dimension to be determined by the eventual behavior of the family on subspaces.
SOURCE Jiahan Du, “Restricted Marstrand's projection theorem for general families of linear subspaces”, arXiv:2510.16671 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16671

ID 371031 The closed-form conjecture for the optimal Schatten norm constant for m matrices LINE 180
Let $M_n(\mathbb C)=\mathbb C^{n\times n}$, and for $A\in M_n(\mathbb C)$ write $|A|=(A^*A)^{1/2}$. For $p>1$ and $m\geq2$, let $c_p(m)$ be the smallest number such that

$$
\left\|\sum_{k=1}^m A_k\right\|_p\leq c_p(m)\left\|\sum_{k=1}^m|A_k|\right\|_p
$$

for all $A_1,\dots,A_m\in M_n(\mathbb C)$. **The closed-form conjecture.** If $x_{p,m}>0$ is the positive solution of

$$
x^p-2x-(m-1)=0,
$$

then

$$
c_p(m)=\frac{\sqrt{x_{p,m}(x_{p,m}+m-1)}}{\left(x_{p,m}^{\,p}+m-1\right)^{1/p}}.
$$

This conjecture extends Lee's problem from two matrices to an arbitrary number of matrices and is motivated by numerical experiments. The paper notes that the formula recovers the known cases $p=1,2,\infty$, while the optimal value for general $p>1$ is the unresolved issue addressed by the conjecture.
SOURCE Quanyu Tang and Shu Zhang, “Generalizing Lee's conjecture on the sum of absolute values of matrices”, arXiv:2510.16846 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16846

ID 371066 The Christofides bound conjecture for P₄-intersecting families LINE 214
Let $P_4$ denote the path on four vertices, and let a $P_4$-intersecting family be a family of subgraphs of $K_n$ such that the edge intersection of any two members contains a copy of $P_4$. An **extremal family** is one having maximum possible size.

**Christofides bound conjecture.** For $n \ge 6$, the maximum size of a $P_4$-intersecting family is

$$
\frac{17}{128} \cdot 2^{\binom n2}.
$$

Furthermore, any extremal family is isomorphic to one generated by the Christofides construction.

The conjecture asks whether the Christofides construction gives the optimal density and classifies all extremal families. The source states that its optimality remains open and notes its connection with the Alon–Spencer conjecture.
SOURCE Paul Hamrick and Gary Hu, “Lower Bounds on Intersection Families for Certain Graphs”, arXiv:2510.18064 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18064

ID 371074 Multiplicative monotonicity of the circular sorting defect LINE 222
Let $t(n)$ be the maximum number of transpositions needed to sort a circular permutation of size $n$, and define the **circular sorting defect** by

$$
c(n)=n-t(n).
$$

**Multiplicative monotonicity conjecture.** Is

$$
c(nm)\geq c(n)
$$

for all integers $n,m\geq 2$?

This asks whether multiplying the size can never decrease the defect relative to the original size. The paper presents it as a different possible notion of monotonicity, and its status remains open.
SOURCE Paul Bastide, Anurag Bishnoi, Carla Groenland, Dion Gijswijt and Rohinee Joshi, “Circular sorting, strong complete mappings and wreath product constructions”, arXiv:2510.18529 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18529

ID 371105 Erdős' high-girth Steiner triple system conjecture LINE 253
A **$(j,i)$-configuration** in a family of sets is a collection of $i$ members spanning at most $j$ ground-set elements; the girth of a triangle packing is the smallest $i\geq 2$ for which it contains an $(i+2,i)$-configuration. **Erdős' conjecture.** For every integer $g\geq 3$, every sufficiently large $K_3$-divisible complete graph admits a $K_3$-decomposition with girth at least $g$. The source states that Kwan, Sah, Sawhney, and Simkin proved this conjecture in full, so it is solved.
SOURCE Luke Postle, “Refined Absorption: A New Proof of the Existence Conjecture and its Applications to Extremal and Probabilistic Design Theory”, arXiv:2510.19978 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19978

ID 371134 Thomas's multirank conjecture for linearizations LINE 281
Let $\mathscr{P}$ be a finite poset, and let $B_1$ and $B_2$ be $\mathscr{P}$-persistence sets. Write $L_{B_1}$ and $L_{B_2}$ for their linearizations, and let $\mathrm{multirank}_{L_B}(S_1,S_2)$ denote the multirank associated with a pair of slices $(S_1,S_2)$. **Thomas's multirank conjecture.** If

$$
\mathrm{multirank}_{L_{B_1}}(S_1,S_2)=\mathrm{multirank}_{L_{B_2}}(S_1,S_2)
$$

for all pairs of slices $(S_1,S_2)$, then

$$
L_{B_1}\cong L_{B_2}.
$$

The statement asserts that multirank invariants completely determine the linearizations of finite-poset persistence sets. In the paper it is presented as Thomas's conjecture and is proved for the setting under consideration, so the claim is solved.
SOURCE Calin Chindris, Min Hyeok Kang and Daniel Kline, “The Jordan type of a multiparameter persistence module”, arXiv:2510.22116 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22116

ID 371183 The additive three-action friendly-versus-antagonistic discrepancy criterion LINE 329
Let $S=\{s_3,s_2,s_1\}$ with $s_3<s_2<s_1$, and suppose the largest subtraction is additive:

$$
s_1=s_3+s_2.
$$

Let a friendly/antagonistic discrepancy mean a difference between the FvF and AvA self-interest games. **Additive discrepancy conjecture.** There exists a heap with a friendly/antagonistic discrepancy if and only if

$$
\frac{s_2}{s_3}\neq k
$$

for every $k\in\mathbb{N}$. When this holds, the first such heap is

$$
x=(i+2)s_2+is_1=is_3+(2i+2)s_2,
$$

where $i\in\mathbb{N}$ satisfies

$$
i+1>\frac{s_2}{s_3}>i.
$$

The statement is based on the paper's computational investigation and is presented without a proof.
SOURCE Anjali Bhagat, Tanmay Kulkarni, Urban Larsson and Divya Murali, “Tie-breaking in self interest cumulative subtraction games”, arXiv:2510.24280 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.24280

ID 371279 The motivic multiple multiplicativity defect bound LINE 424
Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $n$ equipped with a CK decomposition. For an integer $m\geq 2$, an $m$-fold $r$-multiplicative CK decomposition is one whose $(m+1)$-fold intersection product respects the CK grading up to defect $r$; denote the smallest such defect by $\mathfrak{d}_m(X)$. **The motivic multiplicativity defect conjecture.** Any CK decomposition of $X$ is $m$-fold $mn$-multiplicative for every integer $m\geq 2$. In particular, 
$$
\mathfrak{d}_m(X)\leq mn.
$$
 This predicts a sharp linear bound on the obstruction to compatibility between multiple intersection products and CK decompositions; the paper presents it as a first step toward determining these defects, and no resolution is supplied.
SOURCE Ze Xu, “Motivic multiplicativity of complete intersections”, arXiv:2511.01362 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01362

ID 371291 Majority neighbor sum distinguishing conjecture LINE 436
Let $G$ be a graph with minimum degree $\delta(G)\geq 2$, and let $\chi_{\sum}^{M}(G)$ denote its majority neighbor sum distinguishing index. **Majority neighbor sum distinguishing conjecture.** Every graph $G$ with $\delta(G)\geq 2$ satisfies

$$
\chi_{\sum}^{M}(G)\leq 5.
$$

This conjecture is posed after bounds for majority neighbor sum distinguishing colorings, including a bound of 15 for graphs of minimum degree at least 4 and 12 for even-order graphs whose vertices all have even degree; its resolution is not given in the supplied text.
SOURCE Rafał Kalinowski, Monika Pilśniak, Elżbieta Sidorowicz and Elżbieta Turowska, “Quasi-majority neighbor sum distinguishing edge-colorings”, arXiv:2511.01835 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01835

ID 371349 Kierstead-Kostochka-Mydlarz-Szemerédi algorithmic Ore-type conjecture LINE 494
Let an **equitable $k$-coloring** be a proper $k$-coloring whose color classes have sizes differing by at most one, and let $d(x)$ denote the degree of a vertex $x$. **Kierstead-Kostochka-Mydlarz-Szemerédi conjecture.** If $G$ is a graph with $d(x)+d(y)<2k$ for all $xy∈E(G)$, then there exists a polynomial-time algorithm to determine an equitable $k$-coloring of $G$.

This is the algorithmic counterpart of the Ore-type equitable-coloring conjectures. The source says it holds when $k$ is at least a fixed positive proportion of the order of $G$, while smaller values are not covered by the paper.
SOURCE Yangyang Cheng, Zhenyu Li, Wanting Sun and Guanghui Wang, “A step toward Chen-Lih-Wu conjecture”, arXiv:2511.03957 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03957

ID 371360 Reconfiguration conjecture for multipartite hypergraphs LINE 505
Let $H$ be an $r$-partite $r$-graph, let $A$ be one of its $r$ partition classes, and let $\mathbf{RG}_{\mathrm{Mat}}(H,A;k)$ denote the matching reconfiguration graph on matchings of size $k$. Let $\tau(H)$ be the minimum vertex-cover size. **Reconfiguration conjecture.** For $r\geq 2$, if

$$
k<\frac{\tau(H)}{r-1},
$$

then

$$
\mathbf{RG}_{\mathrm{Mat}}(H,A;k)
$$

is connected. This extends the proved $r=3$ reconfiguration result in the spirit of the Henderson–Ryser conjecture. The claim is stated as a conjecture for all uniformities $r\geq 2$; no resolution is supplied in the source.
SOURCE Ronen Wdowinski, “Hall's theorem for reconfigurations and higher dimensional topological connectedness”, arXiv:2511.04863 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04863

ID 371383 Mader's connectivity-keeping tree conjecture LINE 528
Throughout, graphs are finite, simple, and undirected; $\k$ and $\m$ are positive integers, the order of a graph is its number of vertices, and $\kappa(G)$ denotes its connectivity. A graph is $\k$-connected when $\kappa(G)\geq k$. A tree has order $\m$ when it has $\m$ vertices.

**Mader's conjecture.** For any tree $\T$ of order $\m$, every $\k$-connected graph $\G$ with minimum degree

$$
\delta(G)\geq \left\lfloor\frac{3k}{2}\right\rfloor+m-1
$$

contains a subtree $\T'\cong T$ such that

$$
\kappa(G-V(T'))\geq k.
$$

Mader proved the corresponding assertion for a path, and later obtained a weaker quadratic minimum-degree bound for arbitrary trees. The conjecture is known for $\k\leq 3$ and remains open for $\k\geq 4$; a linear minimum-degree sufficient condition is known.
SOURCE Hojin Chu, Shinya Fujita, Boram Park and Homoon Ryu, “Connectivity keeping trees in triangle-free graphs”, arXiv:2511.06622 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06622

ID 371418 Favaron–Shi minimum-degree conjecture for minimal factor-critical graphs LINE 563
Let $G$ be a $k$-factor-critical graph, meaning that deleting any $k$ vertices leaves a graph with a perfect matching. Call $G$ **minimal** if deleting any edge produces a graph that is not $k$-factor-critical. Write $(G)$ for the minimum vertex degree of $G$.

**Favaron–Shi's conjecture.** If $G$ is a minimal $k$-factor-critical graph, then

$$
\delta(G)=k+1.
$$

The conjecture was posed by Favaron and Shi and concerns the sharpness of the general lower bound on the minimum degree of minimal $k$-factor-critical graphs. It is known for several values of $k$, including $k=1$, $k\in\{n-6,n-4,n-2\}$, and $k\in\{2,n-10,n-8\}$, as well as for claw-free graphs; the source proves it for planar graphs.
SOURCE Qiuli Li, Fuliang Lu and Heping Zhang, “On minimal k-factor-critical planar graphs”, arXiv:2511.08137 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08137

ID 371439 Tait's conjecture on minimal crossing diagrams of links LINE 583
Let $L$ be a link. A diagram of $L$ is **reduced alternating** if it is alternating and has no nugatory crossings. The crossing number of $L$ is the minimum number of crossings among all diagrams of $L$.

**Tait's conjecture.** A reduced alternating diagram of $L$ has minimal crossing number, and if $L$ is prime then no non-alternating diagram has minimal crossing number.

This conjecture initiated the study of minimal crossing diagrams of links and was proved independently by Thistlethwaite, Kauffman, and Murasugi in 1987.
SOURCE Erica Flapan and Hugh Howards, “Minimal crossing diagrams of spatial graphs”, arXiv:2511.09712 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09712

ID 371447 The exceptional-order conjecture for decomposing complete graphs into squares of Hamilton cycles LINE 591
Let $K_n$ be the complete graph on $n$ vertices, let $C_n$ be a Hamilton cycle on these vertices, and let $C_n^2$ denote its square, obtained by joining pairs at distance at most two on the cycle. A **square-of-Hamilton-cycle decomposition conjecture.** For any $n \equiv 1 \pmod 4$, $n \not= 9$, there is a decomposition of $E(K_n)$ into copies of $C_n^2$. This strengthens the expected divisibility condition for packing squares of Hamilton cycles and is intended to guide explicit constructions; the cases $n=21,25,33,45,49,57,65,69$ are known, while $n=73$ is described as the smallest unresolved case.
SOURCE Olaf Parczyk, Silas Rathke and Tibor Szabó, “The maximum diameter of 2-dimensional simplicial complexes”, arXiv:2511.10144 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10144

ID 371448 Polynomial Hirsch conjecture for simplicial-complex diameter LINE 592
Let $H_s(n,d)$ denote the maximum diameter of a $d$-dimensional simplicial complex on $n$ vertices. The **simplicial-complex diameter conjecture.** For every $d\geq 3$, there is a threshold $n_0(d)$ such that, for all $n\geq n_0(d)$,

$$
H_s(n,d) = \left\lfloor \frac 1d \cdot \binom{n}{d} - \frac{d+1}{d} \right\rfloor.
$$

This asserts that the trivial upper bound is asymptotically tight in every dimension $d\geq 3$ and would determine the maximum diameter for all sufficiently large numbers of vertices. The source says that the detailed proof of the lower-bound strategy appears in a separate manuscript, while the matching upper-bound conjecture remains open.
SOURCE Olaf Parczyk, Silas Rathke and Tibor Szabó, “The maximum diameter of 2-dimensional simplicial complexes”, arXiv:2511.10144 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10144

ID 371486 Infinitude of prime and lucky numbers of every fractional order LINE 629
Under the restricted factorization described in the source, assign a **fractional order** $\operatorname{ord}(p)$ or $\operatorname{ord}(l)$ recursively to primes $p$ and lucky numbers $l$: lucky primes have order $1$, products of powers of lucky primes have prime order $1/2$, and products of primes have lucky order $1/2$; in general, the order is determined by the smallest fractional order of the relevant factors as specified by the recursive definition. **Fractional-order infinitude conjecture.** For every fixed positive integer $n$, there are infinitely many prime numbers or lucky numbers of order $1/n$. This generalizes the conjecture that there are infinitely many lucky primes, but the source gives no resolution.
SOURCE Marthinus Michael Dreeckmeier, “On the Fundamental Arithmetical Structure and Distribution of Lucky Numbers”, arXiv:2511.11657 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11657

ID 371499 The Oka-1 conjecture for rationally connected projective manifolds LINE 642
Let $X$ be a projective manifold that is **rationally connected**, meaning that any two points of $X$ can be joined by a rational curve. Let Oka-1 denote the property that, for every open Riemann surface, compact Runge set, discrete interpolation sequence, and continuous map holomorphic near the relevant interpolation data, one can find a homotopic holomorphic map with the prescribed approximation and jet interpolation. **The Oka-1 conjecture.** Every rationally connected projective manifold is Oka-1. This is posed among open problems about weak Oka-1 manifolds; the source provides no resolution of the conjecture.
SOURCE Yunling Chen, John Erik Fornæss and Song-Yan Xie, “Generating all Ahlfors currents by a single entire curve”, arXiv:2511.12473 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12473

ID 371525 Exact induced-degenerate-subgraph ratio conjecture for k-degenerate graphs LINE 668
Let $\alpha_d(k)$ denote the infimum, over $k$-degenerate graphs, of the ratio of the order of a largest induced $d$-degenerate subgraph to the number of vertices. **Induced-degenerate-subgraph ratio conjecture for $k\geq 3$.** For all integers $k\geq 3$ and $d$ with $k>d$,

$$
\alpha_d(k)=\frac{d+1}{k+1}.
$$

This assertion is motivated by computational experiments and would determine the exact ratios for the indicated parameters. The source presents it as open.
SOURCE Alexander Clow, Sean Kim and Ladislav Stacho, “A Note on Large Degenerate Induced Subgraphs in Sparse Graphs”, arXiv:2511.13693 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13693

ID 371527 Induced forest bound for graphs of prescribed girth LINE 670
Let $G$ be a graph on $n$ vertices with $m$ edges, and let $\alpha_1(G)$ denote the order of a largest induced $1$-degenerate subgraph of $G$, equivalently a largest induced forest. **Induced forest girth conjecture.** For every integer $k\geq 3$, if $G$ has girth at least $k$, then

$$
\alpha_1(G)\geq n-\frac{m}{k}.
$$

The source notes that the bound would be best possible, as witnessed by a disjoint union of cycles of length $k$, and that the case of girth $3$ is trivial. The general assertion is presented as open.
SOURCE Alexander Clow, Sean Kim and Ladislav Stacho, “A Note on Large Degenerate Induced Subgraphs in Sparse Graphs”, arXiv:2511.13693 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13693

ID 371634 Hahn–Hell–Poljak conjecture for odd wheels LINE 777
Let $W_t$ be the join of the cycle $C_t$ with $K_1$, and let $d4b0(G)$ denote the ultimate independence ratio of a graph $G$. For odd wheels, $d4b0(W_{2t+1})$ is bounded below by $1/4$ because $d4b0(G)\geq 1/\chi(G)$ and $\chi(W_{2t+1})=4$. **Hahn–Hell–Poljak's conjecture.** For all integers $t\geq 1$, 
$$
\mathscr{I}(W_{2t+1})=\frac{1}{4}.
$$
 Determining the ultimate independence ratio of odd wheels is a central open problem in this setting; the $5$-wheel is identified as the smallest graph whose ratio is unknown.
SOURCE Alexander Clow, Hitesh Kumar and Shivaramakrishna Pragada, “Improved Bounds for the Ultimate Independence Ratio of Odd Wheels”, arXiv:2511.18747 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18747

ID 371635 Conjecture on the largest 3-colourable subgraph of odd-wheel squares LINE 778
Let $W_t$ be the join of the cycle $C_t$ with $K_1$, let $\alpha(G)$ be the independence number of $G$, and let $G\Box H$ denote the Cartesian product of graphs. For $t\geq 2$, consider the square $W_{2t+1}^{\Box 2}$ and its product with $K_3$. **Odd-wheel square subgraph conjecture.** For all $t\geq 2$, 
$$
(2t+2)\alpha(W_{2t+1})-\alpha(W_{2t+1}^{\Box 2}\Box K_3)=t-1.
$$
 Hence, the largest $3$-colourable subgraph of $W_{2t+1}^{\Box 2}$ has order $4t^2+5t+3$. This is motivated by computational observations and is presented as a future-work conjecture; no resolution is supplied.
SOURCE Alexander Clow, Hitesh Kumar and Shivaramakrishna Pragada, “Improved Bounds for the Ultimate Independence Ratio of Odd Wheels”, arXiv:2511.18747 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18747

ID 371692 Conjecture on the minimum size of uniqueness sets for the Ising model LINE 835
Let $u(k,q)$ denote the size of the smallest set of uniqueness for the positive function class $\left(\mathcal{B}^{k}_{q}\right)_+$. In particular, consider the case $q=2$. **Minimum-size uniqueness conjecture.**

$$
u(k,2)=k+1,
$$

i.e., for every $k$ there are no sets of uniqueness having at most $k$ elements.
This conjecture concerns the exact minimum cardinality of a set of uniqueness in the $q=2$ case; the preceding results establish corresponding bounds, while the equality asserted here remains unresolved in the supplied text.
SOURCE Tomasz Skalski and Tomasz Stroiński, “Level sets and maximum likelihood estimation for the Ising model”, arXiv:2511.20925 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20925

ID 371753 The universal $L^2$-torsion characterization of free-group isomorphisms LINE 895
Let $F_1$ and $F_2$ be finitely generated free groups, let $\varphi\colon F_1\to F_2$ be a homomorphism, and let $\tau^{(2)}_u(\varphi)$ denote its universal $L^2$-torsion. **Universal $L^2$-torsion conjecture.** The homomorphism $\varphi$ is an isomorphism if and only if

$$
\tau^{(2)}_u(\varphi)=1.
$$

The conjecture seeks to determine how much group-theoretic information universal $L^2$-torsion detects. The source notes that Jaikin-Zapirain proved an $L^2$-homology characterization in the equal-rank injective case via compressed subgroups, while the stated equivalence for arbitrary homomorphisms between finitely generated free groups remains unresolved.
SOURCE Jianru Duan, “Universal L^2-torsion and sutured decomposition for 3-manifolds”, arXiv:2512.01305 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01305

ID 371827 Stein's minimum semi-degree conjecture for oriented paths LINE 968
Let $D$ be an oriented graph, let $k$ be a positive integer, and write $\delta^{0}(D)$ for the minimum semi-degree. An **oriented path of length $k$** is a path on $k+1$ vertices with an arbitrary orientation of its edges.

**Stein's conjecture.** If

$$
\delta^{0}(D)>k/2,
$$

then $D$ contains every oriented path of length $k$.

This is the explicitly labelled conjecture introduced after earlier results on long directed paths and cycles. The supplied text gives no resolution status for it.
SOURCE Bin Chen, Xinmin Hou and Xinyu Zhou, “Paths with two blocks in oriented graphs of large minimum semi-degree”, arXiv:2512.04423 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04423

ID 371961 Non-amenable image criterion for infinitesimal containment of compact actions LINE 1099
Let $G$ be a compact group with Haar probability measure $\mu$, let $\rho\colon\Gamma\to G$ be a homomorphism, and let $\Gamma$ act on $(G,\mu)$ by left translation through $\rho$. **Non-amenable image criterion.** If $\rho(\Lambda)$ is open in $G$ for every non-amenable subgroup $\Lambda$ of $\Gamma$, then 
$$
(G,\mu)\xhookrightarrow{\operatorname{inf}}\Gamma.
$$
 This is stated as a special case of the preceding compact-action conjecture and is presented as an open question supported only by the authors' inability to find counterexamples.
SOURCE Mikołaj Frączyk, “Infinitesimal containment and sparse factors of iid”, arXiv:2512.09301 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09301

ID 371966 Schwarzian norm conjecture for maximizers of the functional Lambda LINE 1104
Let $\mathcal{S}$ be the class of normalized univalent functions, let $\overline{\mathbf{S}}$ be the indicated closed Schwarzian space, let $S_f$ be the Schwarzian derivative of $f\in\mathcal{S}$, and let $\Lambda_\alpha$ be the functional in the source. **Lambda-maximizer Schwarzian norm conjecture.** For every $\alpha>0$, if $\Lambda_\alpha$ attains at least one maximum at $S_f\in\overline{\mathbf{S}}$ for some $f\in\mathcal{S}$, then

$$
\|S_f\|_{E_2}=6.
$$

The preceding text asks separately whether maxima exist; this conjecture concerns the norm of any such maximizer under the stated attainment hypothesis.
SOURCE Jianjun Jin, “Complex exponential integral means spectra of univalent functions and the Brennan conjecture”, arXiv:2512.09330 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09330

ID 371970 The hereditary-family polychromatic coloring conjecture LINE 1108
Let $\mathcal F$ be a hereditary family of hypergraphs. For $k\ge1$, let $m_k(\mathcal F)$ be the smallest integer $m$ such that every $m$-heavy hypergraph in $\mathcal F$ has a polychromatic $k$-coloring, with $m_k(\mathcal F)=\infty$ if no such integer exists. **Hereditary-family polychromatic coloring conjecture.** If

$$
m_2(\mathcal F)<\infty,
$$

then

$$
m_k(\mathcal F)<\infty
$$

for every $k\ge2$. The conjecture is known when $m_2(\mathcal F)=2$, in which case Berge proved $m_k(\mathcal F)=k$ for every $k$; it is open in general, and the hereditary hypothesis is necessary.
SOURCE Gábor Damásdi, Balázs Keszegh, János Pach, Dömötör Pálvölgyi and Géza Tóth, “Coloring Geometric Hypergraphs: A Survey”, arXiv:2512.09509 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09509

ID 372116 Lehmer-type conjecture for adelic heights above the energy minimum LINE 1252
Let $K$ be a number field, let $\mu$ be an adelic measure defined over $K$, let $h_\mu$ be its associated height on $\mathbb{P}^1(\overline{K})$, and let $\mathcal{L}(\mu)$ denote the energy infimum associated with $\mu$. **Lehmer-type conjecture above the energy minimum.** There exists $\varepsilon>0$ such that

$$
[K(\alpha):K]\bigl(h_\mu(\alpha)-\mathcal{L}(\mu)\bigr)\geq\varepsilon
$$

for every $\alpha\in\mathbb{P}^1(\overline{K})$ such that $h_\mu(\alpha)>\mathcal{L}(\mu)$.

This formulation is designed to remain meaningful when $\mathcal{L}(\mu)>0$. The supplied text subsequently discusses evidence that it fails for particular measures, but does not provide a resolution of the general assertion.
SOURCE Preston Kelley, “Areal Weil Heights”, arXiv:2512.16007 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16007

ID 372164 Four-dichotomic-qubit compatibility-degree conjecture LINE 1299
Let $s_{\mathbb C}(2,4)$ denote the minimum compatibility degree of four dichotomic measurements on a complex qubit. **Four-dichotomic-qubit compatibility-degree conjecture.**

$$
s_{\mathbb C}(2,4)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{13}}.
$$

The value is attained by the displayed computational-basis and equiangular-line measurements, and numerical optimization provides evidence for optimality; a proof remains open.
SOURCE Andreas Bluhm, Eric Evert, Igor Klep, Victor Magron and Ion Nechita, “Inclusion constants for free spectrahedra with applications to quantum incompatibility”, arXiv:2512.17706 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17706

ID 372246 Dhawan's multipartite hypergraph choosability conjecture LINE 1381
Let $k\geqslant 2$. A **$k$-partite $k$-graph** is a $k$-uniform hypergraph whose vertex set has a partition into $k$ parts such that every edge contains exactly one vertex from each part. Let $H$ be such a hypergraph with maximum degree at most $\Delta$, and let $\mathsf{ch}(H)$ denote its choice number.

**Dhawan's conjecture.** For every $k\geqslant 2$, there is a constant $c=c(k)>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $\Delta$,

$$
\mathsf{ch}(H)\leqslant c\log \Delta.
$$

This is a stronger uniform statement extending the bipartite-graph conjecture to all uniformities. A recent bound of order $(\Delta/\log\Delta)^{1/(k-1)}$ is known, but the conjectured logarithmic bound remains unresolved.
SOURCE Peter Bradshaw, Abhishek Dhawan, Nhi Dinh, Shlok Mulye and Rohan Rathi, “Choosability of multipartite hypergraphs”, arXiv:2512.21222 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21222

ID 372253 Stability conjecture for packed words in the plactic monoid LINE 1388
A word $u$ has maximum letter $m$ and is **$m$-packed** if it contains at least one copy of every integer in $[m]=\{1,\ldots,m\}$. A word $u$ is **$m$-stable** when its centralizers satisfy $C(u^k)=C(u^{k+1})$ for the relevant stability range $m\le k$. **Stability conjecture.** If $u$ is $m$-packed, then $u$ is $m$-stable. This is supported by computations for $m=3,4$ and words of length at most $8$; the cases $m=1$ and $m=2$ were already established, but the general assertion remains open.
SOURCE Bruce E. Sagan and Chenchen Zhao, “Properties of plactic monoid centralizers”, arXiv:2512.21401 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21401

ID 372267 The divisibility conjecture for equiconstructible equilateral triangles LINE 1402
Let $(a,b,c)$ be the side lengths of the triangular tile, and say that a positive real number $X$ is **equiconstructible** by $(a,b,c)$ when an equilateral triangle with side length $X$ can be tiled by copies of that triangle. **Divisibility conjecture.** All equiconstructible $X$ are divisible by $ab$; in particular, for the tile $(a,b,c)=(5,16,19)$, every equiconstructible $X$ is divisible by $16$.

The preceding lemma proves the divisibility statement when $a$ and $b$ are square-free, while the case $(5,16,19)$ is presented as the smallest interesting case beyond that result. The conjecture is intended to constrain the possible side lengths of equilateral tilings.
SOURCE Yan X Zhang, “Tiling Triangles with 2π/3 Angles”, arXiv:2512.22696 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22696

ID 372356 Erdős's eventual-time conjecture for quadratic character sums LINE 1491
Let $
obreak\footcomment$ be omitted. For an odd prime $p$, write

$$
S_\ell(p):=\sum_{n\leq \ell}\left(\frac{n}{p}\right),
$$

where $\left(\frac{\cdot}{p}\right)$ is the Legendre symbol modulo $p$. For $\varepsilon>0$, let $F_\varepsilon(p)$ be the smallest integer such that

$$
S_\ell(p)<\varepsilon\ell\qquad\text{for every }\ell\geq F_\varepsilon(p).
$$

**Erdős's eventual-time conjecture.** There exists a constant $C_\varepsilon>0$ such that

$$
\sum_{p\leq x}F_\varepsilon(p)\sim C_\varepsilon\,\frac{x}{\log x}.
$$

Erdős posed this as a question about the eventual time at which a quadratic character sum remains below a linear barrier. The conjecture was proved by Elliott.
SOURCE Quanyu Tang and Hao Zhang, “Average first-passage times for character sums”, arXiv:2512.24631 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24631

ID 372358 The exact edge-count conjecture for order-7 triangular tiling spirals LINE 1493
Let $e_{d(7)}(n)$ denote the maximum number of edges in a penny graph with $n$ vertices and distance $d(7)$. **Exact edge-count conjecture.** For all $n
obreak\geq 1$, $e_{d(7)}(n)$ equals the upper bound from Theorem~. This conjecture asserts exactness of the proved upper bound for the order-$7$ triangular tiling; the supplied text gives no resolution beyond the stated upper bound and partial computational evidence.
SOURCE Ádám Sagmeister and Konrad J. Swanepoel, “Penny graphs in the hyperbolic plane”, arXiv:2512.24832 (2025). https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24832

ID 372391 Linear upper bound for set mappings of uniform hypergraphs LINE 1526
Let $k$ be a positive integer and let $G$ be a $k$-uniform hypergraph with $m$ edges and no isolated vertices. The parameter $w(G)$ is the minimum $N$ such that every map

$$
f:E(K_N^{(k)})\to E(K_N^{(k)})
$$

with $f(e)\cap e=\emptyset$ for every $e\in E(K_N^{(k)})$ admits a copy $G^*$ of $G$ in $K_N^{(k)}$ satisfying $f(e)\cap V(G^*)=\emptyset$ for every $e\in E(G^*)$. **Hypergraph linear-bound conjecture.** For every such $k$-graph $G$, one has

$$
w(G)=O(m).
$$

This would extend the paper's linear upper bound for graphs to uniform hypergraphs, but the authors note that their methods do not easily generalize to this setting.
SOURCE Lior Gishboliner, Zhihan Jin and Benny Sudakov, “Set mappings for general graphs”, arXiv:2601.00766 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00766

ID 372399 The Delta Conjecture for maximum nullity of graphs LINE 1534
Let $G$ be a simple graph, and let $\delta(G)$ denote its minimum degree. The maximum nullity $\mathrm{M}(G)$ is the greatest nullity among matrices associated with $G$ in the maximum nullity problem. **Delta Conjecture.** For every simple graph $G$,

$$
\delta(G) \le \mathrm{M}(G).
$$

The conjecture arose in combinatorial matrix theory and is a lower bound on maximum nullity. It is known for all bipartite graphs and for complements of bipartite graphs, but the source does not report a complete resolution.
SOURCE H. Tracy Hall, “The Delta Theorem: a dimension bound for faithful orthogonal graph representations”, arXiv:2601.01211 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01211

ID 372400 Strong Delta Conjecture for the SAP maximum nullity parameter LINE 1535
Let $G$ be a simple graph, and let $\delta(G)$ denote its minimum degree. Write $\nu(G)$ for the greatest codimension of a faithful orthogonal representation whose Gram matrix has the Strong Arnold Property (SAP). **Strong Delta Conjecture.** For every simple graph $G$,

$$
\delta(G) \le \nu(G).
$$

This conjecture would strengthen Maehara's conjecture because $\nu(G)\leq \mathrm{M}_+(G)$. The source introduces it as a possible stronger conjecture and does not report a resolution.
SOURCE H. Tracy Hall, “The Delta Theorem: a dimension bound for faithful orthogonal graph representations”, arXiv:2601.01211 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01211

ID 372436 Subadditivity in t for all-symbol PIR and batch codes LINE 1570
Let $ASP(k,t,q)$ and $ASB(k,t,q)$ denote the minimum lengths of, respectively, all-symbol PIR and all-symbol batch codes with parameters $k,t,q$. For all $k,q$ and $t_1,t_2\geq 1$, **Subadditivity in $t$.**

$$
ASP(k,t_1+t_2,q)\leq ASP(k,t_1,q)+ASP(k,t_2,q),
$$

$$
ASB(k,t_1+t_2,q)\leq ASB(k,t_1,q)+ASB(k,t_2,q).
$$

The claim extends the known subadditivity results for functional PIR and batch codes; the paper notes that the corresponding general statement for all-symbol parameters remains open.
SOURCE Avital Boruchovsky, Anina Gruica, Jonathan Niemann and Eitan Yaakobi, “Serving Every Symbol: All-Symbol PIR and Batch Codes”, arXiv:2601.04041 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04041

ID 372437 The forbidden-configuration bound for binary matrices LINE 1571
Let $t$ be given, and let ${\mathrm{forb}}(m,F(0,2^{t-2}+1,1,0))$ denote the maximum number of columns in an $m$-rowed binary matrix avoiding the configuration $F(0,2^{t-2}+1,1,0)$. **Forbidden-configuration bound conjecture.**

$$
{\mathrm{forb}}(m,F(0,2^{t-2}+1,1,0))\leq \frac{2^{t}-1}{t}m+1,
$$

with equality when $m\equiv 0\pmod{t}$. The conjecture is motivated by constructions using the matrix $K_t$ and the matrices $B_i=K_t\backslash \mathbf{1}_t$, which attain the stated bound in the displayed cases; its validity for arbitrary $t$ is not established in the supplied text.
SOURCE Richard P. Anstee, Oakley Edens, Arvin Sahami, Jaehwan Seok and Attila Sali, “Exact Bounds for Forbidden Configurations and the Extremal Matrices”, arXiv:2601.04084 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04084

ID 372500 Polynomial anticoncentration conjecture under a minimum-degree condition LINE 1633
Let $d_0,c>0$ be universal constants. For every integer $d\geq d_0$, let $G$ be a connected $n$-vertex graph with minimum degree at least $d$, and let $\mathcal{T}$ be a uniformly random spanning tree of $G$. For every $n$-vertex tree $T$, **Minimum-degree anticoncentration conjecture.**

$$
\Pr\bigl[\mathcal{T}\simeq_{\mathrm{iso}}T\bigr]\leq n^{-c}.
$$

This proposes a polynomial anticoncentration bound under the minimum-degree assumption, which is substantially weaker than almost regularity. Complete bipartite graphs show that the paper's stronger exponential conclusions fail under this weaker hypothesis, but the authors note that such graphs still contain polynomially many pairwise non-isomorphic spanning trees.
SOURCE Hyunwoo Lee, “Anticoncentration of random spanning trees in almost regular graphs”, arXiv:2601.07740 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07740

ID 372570 Ebrahimi–Mohar–Nikiforov–Ahmady conjecture on the spectral sum of graphs LINE 1701
Let $G$ be any graph of order $n$, and let $\lambda_1(G)$ and $\lambda_2(G)$ denote its two largest adjacency eigenvalues. **Ebrahimi–Mohar–Nikiforov–Ahmady conjecture.** The spectral sum satisfies

$$
\lambda_1(G)+\lambda_2(G)\leq \frac{8n}{7}.
$$

The conjecture extends the extremal spectral-sum question from trees to arbitrary graphs. The supplied context states that analogous questions for graphs remain open and gives no resolution of this bound.
SOURCE Hitesh Kumar, Bojan Mohar, Shivaramakrishna Pragada and Hanmeng Zhan, “Convex combination of first and second eigenvalues of trees”, arXiv:2601.10036 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10036

ID 372617 Circular-flow threshold conjecture for two-dimensional normed flows LINE 1748
Let $G$ be a graph and let a **circular $(3+\epsilon)$-NZF** mean a circular nowhere-zero flow with parameter $3+\epsilon$. **Circular-flow threshold conjecture.** For $\epsilon\in[0,1]$, there exist two functions $f$ and $g$, with $f(\epsilon)\in[2,+\infty]$ strictly increasing and $g(\epsilon)\in[1,2]$ strictly decreasing, such that if $G$ admits a circular $(3+\epsilon)$-NZF, then

$$
\phi_{2,p}(G)=2 \quad \text{for all } p\ge f(\epsilon) \text{ and for all } 1\le p\le g(\epsilon).
$$

This conjecture seeks parameter ranges in which a circular-flow hypothesis forces the two-dimensional $p$-normed flow index to attain its minimum; no resolution is supplied.
SOURCE Chenxing Li, Jiaao Li, Rong Luo and Bo Su, “High-Dimensional p-Normed Flows”, arXiv:2601.12036 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12036

ID 372639 Normal-form classification conjecture for integrable RH conservation laws LINE 1770
Consider an integrable hierarchy of scalar conservation laws

$$
\frac{\partial u}{\partial t_n}=\partial_x\omega_n,
$$

which is a perturbation of the Riemann--Hopf hierarchy, and transform it to the Arsie--Lorenzoni--Moro normal form

$$
\widetilde{\omega}_1=\frac{w^2}{2}+a(w)w_x+\sum_{k\geq2}\epsilon^k\sum_{\substack{\lambda\in\mathcal{P}_k\\m_1(\lambda)=0}}c_\lambda(w)w_\lambda.
$$

Assume $a(w)=0$ and $c_2(w)\neq0$. **Normal-form classification conjecture.** All coefficients $c_\lambda(w)$ with odd $|\lambda|$ vanish; the functions $c_2(w),c_4(w),\ldots$ uniquely determine every other $c_\lambda(w)$; and $c_2(w),c_4(w),\ldots$ are arbitrary free functional parameters. The source supplies no evidence of resolution for this stated claim.
SOURCE Alexandr Buryak, Jianghao Xu and Di Yang, “Bihamiltonian tests for integrable systems associated to rank-1 F-CohFTs”, arXiv:2601.13203 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13203

ID 372692 Degree-plus-one conjecture for graphs of minimum degree three LINE 1823
For a graph $G$, write $d_G(v)$ for the degree of a vertex $v$. A $({\rm degree}+1)$-list assignment assigns each vertex $v$ a list $L(v)$ with $|L(v)|\geq d_G(v)+1$. A graph is **proper conflict-free $({\rm degree}+1)$-choosable** if it has a proper conflict-free $L$-coloring for every such list assignment. **Degree-plus-one conjecture.** Every connected graph of minimum degree at least $3$ is proper conflict-free $({\rm degree}+1)$-choosable. This seeks a one-color improvement over the general degree-plus bound for graphs without vertices of degree below three, and remains open according to the source.
SOURCE Masaki Kashima, Riste Škrekovski and Rongxing Xu, “Degree-choosability of proper conflict-free list coloring of sparse graphs”, arXiv:2601.15611 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15611

ID 372739 Hurlbert–Kamat leaf-centred maximum-star conjecture for trees LINE 1869
Let $T$ be a tree, let $k\geq 1$, and let $\mathcal{I}_{T}^{k}(v)$ denote the family of independent sets of size $k$ in $T$ containing $v$. A **leaf-centred maximum-star conjecture.** There exists a leaf $\ell$ of $T$ such that

$$
|\mathcal{I}_{T}^{k}(v)|\leq|\mathcal{I}_{T}^{k}(\ell)|
$$

for every $v\in V(T)$. This conjecture was disproved by independent work of Baber and Borg, which produced counterexamples for every $k\geq 5$.
SOURCE Daniel Iľkovič and Jun Yan, “Distribution of independent sets in perfect r-ary trees”, arXiv:2601.16953 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16953

ID 372778 Roberson's weak conjecture for bounded Hadwiger number LINE 1907
For a graph $G$, its **Hadwiger number** is the largest integer $t$ such that $G$ contains the complete graph $K_t$ as a minor. Two graphs are homomorphism indistinguishable over a graph class if they admit the same number of homomorphisms from every graph in that class. **Roberson's weak conjecture.** For every $k \geq 0$, there exist non-isomorphic graphs $G$ and $H$ that are homomorphism indistinguishable over all graphs of Hadwiger number $\leq k$. This is a concrete form of the question whether sparse graph classes can distinguish all non-isomorphic graphs. The paper proves the conjecture for vortex-free graph classes, while the general minor-excluded case remains unresolved.
SOURCE Daniel Neuen and Tim Seppelt, “Distinguishing Graphs by Counting Homomorphisms from Sparse Graphs”, arXiv:2601.18602 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18602

ID 372803 The minimum-fort-size conjecture for marginal observances LINE 1932
Let $G$ be a graph. Write $\underline{f}(G)$ for the minimum fort size of $G$, let $\mathrm{MObs}(G;i)$ denote the marginal observance at step $i$, and let $\gamma_P(G)$ denote the power domination number. For a positive integer $k$, write $[k]=\{1,\ldots,k\}$. **Minimum-fort-size conjecture.** If

$$
\underline{f}(G)\geq 4,
$$

then

$$
\mathrm{MObs}(G;i)\geq 4
$$

for all $i\in[\gamma_P(G)]$. This asks whether the relationship between minimum fort size and marginal observances established for smaller minimum fort sizes extends to minimum fort size at least four; its status is not resolved in the source.
SOURCE Beth Bjorkman, Sean English and Johnathan Koch, “Cost-Benefit Analysis for PMU Placement in Power Grids”, arXiv:2601.19775 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19775

ID 372813 The high-dimensional point-line induced matching conjecture LINE 1942
Let $\mathrm{IM}(d,q)$ denote the maximum size of an induced matching in the point-line incidence graph of $\mathbb{F}_q^d$. **High-dimensional induced matching conjecture.** For every fixed $d\ge 3$, 

$$
\mathrm{IM}(d,q)=o(q^d)\qquad\text{as }q\to\infty.
$$

Beyond the planar case, essentially no nontrivial upper bounds are known for point-line induced matchings in $\mathbb{F}_q^d$ when $d\ge 3$; even a qualitative improvement over the trivial bound $\mathrm{IM}(d,q)\le q^d$ would be significant.
SOURCE Zach Hunter, Cosmin Pohoata, Jacques Verstraete and Shengtong Zhang, “Large point-line matchings and small Nikodym sets”, arXiv:2601.19879 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19879

ID 372814 The prime-field planar induced matching conjecture LINE 1943
Let $\mathrm{IM}(2,q)$ denote the maximum size of an induced matching in the point-line incidence graph of $\mathbb{F}_q^2$. **Prime-field planar induced matching conjecture.** There exists an absolute constant $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large primes $q$,

$$
\mathrm{IM}(2,q)\le q^{3/2-c}.
$$

For arbitrary prime powers, Vinh's finite-field Szemerédi–Trotter theorem gives $\mathrm{IM}(2,q)\le q^{3/2}+q$, and this exponent is sharp up to constants when $q$ is a square because of the Hermitian unital. The conjecture asks for a power-saving improvement over this bound over prime fields.
SOURCE Zach Hunter, Cosmin Pohoata, Jacques Verstraete and Shengtong Zhang, “Large point-line matchings and small Nikodym sets”, arXiv:2601.19879 (2026). https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19879
